# NereusSDR for iPhone and iPad: Implementation Plan

> **Execution:** run with `crew` (`/crew <this file>`) under `cost-aware-execution`.
> Requirements and acceptance cases are binding; test order and review effort follow
> the risk-based policy; UI evidence follows `ui-verification`. Two sessions run this
> one plan, each only its own tasks (Global Constraints, "Who runs which task"). No
> review between tasks; each session ends with one whole-branch review of its branch.

**Goal:** Ship the NereusSDR iPhone and iPad app, with receive and transmit in the first
App Store release, together with every station, desktop and service change it needs, as
one body of work.

**Architecture:** The station (`nereusd`, or the desktop app while it is open) does all
radio and DSP work and speaks one written, versioned link: a TLS WebSocket control
session carrying JSON, plus WebRTC media (Opus audio, NSDC display frames). This plan
writes that link down with a conformance suite both ends run, then gives the station
per-device identity and pairing; the values, lists, Setup descriptions and ported display
computations the phone shows; remote access through a rendezvous and relay; remote
transmit with its whole safety harness (starting with the DSP-control thread R-R3-39
requires); several devices on one Core at once (spec §3.9, which replaced the takeover
question on 2026-09-24) and the transmit-coupled accessory commands; and the desktop's
station switch. The app is a Swift package (`NereusKit`: link, mirror, media,
band) that builds and tests on macOS, plus a SwiftUI and Metal app with a Live Activity
extension, generated by XcodeGen.

**Tech Stack:** Station and desktop: C++20, Qt 6 (Core, Network, WebSockets, Widgets,
Test), CMake and Ninja, OpenSSL 3, libdatachannel 0.24.5 with libjuice, libsrtp and
usrsctp, Opus, libsodium and SPAKE2+EE (new), coturn (server side only). App: Swift 6.3
(6.4 with Xcode 27), SwiftUI, Metal, Network framework, AVFAudio, ActivityKit,
PushToTalk, CoreBluetooth, CryptoKit, swift-testing, XcodeGen; vendored C sources for
Opus, libdatachannel, libjuice, libsrtp, usrsctp, Mbed TLS, libsodium and SPAKE2+EE.

**Spec:** [2026-09-23-iphone-app-design.md](2026-09-23-iphone-app-design.md). Every
decision is settled there (D1 to D42, requirements R-IOS-01 to R-IOS-29); this is how it
gets built. The station documents it cites (the remote design, the pairing design, the
R3 plan, media control, the display codec, notch control) are on `codex/integrate-r2-main`,
read for this plan at `4bb89b5d`.

## Global Constraints

* **Design authority** is the spec, plus, for the station, the remote design
  (`2026-07-28-remote-daemon-architecture-design.md`), the pairing design
  (`2026-08-02-remote-station-identity-and-pairing-design.md`) and the R3 plan
  (`2026-09-20-remote-daemon-r3-plan.md`). Do not relitigate a decision. If the spec is
  wrong or silent on something material, stop and report NEEDS_CONTEXT.
* **Who runs which task** (JJ's decision, 2026-09-24): one plan, one owner per task,
  and each task opens with a **Runs in** line that says which.
  * **Station tasks** (1 to 4, 12 to 14, 16 to 50 without the lettered ones, and 71 to 78)
    run in the Core/GUI session's lanes and reach `codex/integrate-r2-main`. Their build
    directory is the lane's (`build-integration` or `build-lane-b`), and `build` in a
    verification command means that directory.
  * **Phone tasks** (5 to 11, 11a, 15, 15b, 16a, 27a, 28a, 29a, 51 to 69 with their
    lettered tasks, and 70 with JJ) run in
    the phone session on `claude/iphone-app`, cut from `codex/integrate-r2-main` (see
    "Before Task 1"). Its controller merges `codex/integrate-r2-main` into
    `claude/iphone-app` only between tasks and never commits to
    `codex/integrate-r2-main`.
  * A phone task starts only when every station task it consumes has reached
    `codex/integrate-r2-main` and been merged in. Until then it waits, and the controller
    tells JJ which station task it is waiting on; nothing stands in for a missing piece.
  * Each controller's ledger records the other session's tasks as they land
    (`Task N: complete (<session>, <commit>)`) and skips every task it does not own.
  * This plan and its spec change only on `claude/nereussdr-iphone-app-5fb988`, whose
    copy is the authority; the Core/GUI session cherry-picks each plan commit into its
    lanes. Before a station task changes anything a phone task reads (the
    `tests/data/link/v1/` layout, `surface.json`'s top-level keys, `hello`'s `majors`
    and `features`, the version rule), the Core/GUI session tells the phone session.
* **The other agent.** Another agent works in `/Users/j.j.boyd/.codex/`; never open or
  change anything there.
* **Tracing.** Every commit names the requirement IDs it implements at the end of the
  subject line, in the remote branch's style: `(R-IOS-04)`, or `(R-IOS-12, R-IOS-08)`.
  Where a task also touches an R3 requirement, name it too (`R-R3-39`).
* **Protocol versions.** Follow the station branch's rule: each new feature gets its own
  capability version in `StationCapabilities` (`<feature>Version`, the pattern of
  `notchControlVersion` and `audioProfileVersion`), and `kSessionProtocolMinor` never
  changes. Where the station must know a client's support before capabilities are sent
  (device authentication, pairing, several devices at once, Setup descriptions), both ends
  declare features in the `hello` `features` object of Task 4. A client never sends a
  message kind or verb the station has not advertised, because an older station ends the
  connection on anything it cannot decode; an older client logs and ignores an unknown
  kind. Older peers see exactly today's wire (golden comparisons where a test exists).
  Supported majors follow D23 and D39 (Task 4).
* **The link document and the conformance suite move with the wire.** A task that adds or
  changes anything on the wire updates `docs/architecture/2026-09-23-station-link-v1.md`,
  `tests/data/link/v1/` and `surface.json` in the same commit, and the conformance
  runners pass on both ends. That document, as merged from `codex/integrate-r2-main`,
  is the authority for the wire: where this plan describes the wire differently, the
  document wins, and an implementer follows it and names the difference in its report
  so the controller corrects the plan. Its Conformance section defines the fixture
  format both runners implement; the phone's runners follow that section, not any
  description of the format in this plan.
* **The transmit safety boundary.** Nothing lets a remote client key the radio outside the
  station's gates: transmit disabled until snapshot-complete; the watchdog; the
  starvation deadline; the time-out; the unkey-confirmed gate; `TxInterlockPolicy`, PA
  protection and SWR gating authoritative at the station. A reconnect, a snapshot replay,
  a path change or a property write never keys. The tasks that touch this boundary are
  flagged in their execution notes for an earlier independent review.
* **Station code** follows `CLAUDE.md` and `CONTRIBUTING.md`: braces on all control flow;
  no raw `new`/`delete` outside Qt parent ownership; `constexpr`, never `#define`, for
  constants; `m_camelCase` members, `kPascalCase` constants, `PascalCase` classes;
  `qCWarning(lc…)` for errors, never exceptions; `Q_OS_*` platform guards. `AppSettings`,
  never `QSettings`; booleans are `"True"` / `"False"`. Core stays GUI-free (`nereusd`
  links NereusCore only). Cross-thread work uses queued signals; no mutex in an audio
  callback; no network or codec work on the DSP callback (R-R3-06); once Tasks 31 and 32
  land, no WDSP call on an event loop (R-R3-39).
* **Operator wording.** Every string a user reads is plain operator English with no
  protocol, grant, budget, capability, session or snapshot jargon, and no source cites
  inside strings (a cite goes in a comment beside the string). Station-side strings pass
  `OperatorWording::isPlain` (`tests/OperatorWording.h`), and the desktop shows Core
  reasons through `src/gui/OperatorReasonText`, as on the station branch; the phone shows
  the Core's reason text as sent. App text matches the spec's §5 and the board's
  pictures. On screen, in both apps, the NereusSDR computer you connect to, update and
  pair with is "the Core" (D43); "station" appears on screen only in its ham sense (your
  station, the station callsign, the station's network). Code identifiers, wire names,
  the link document and this plan's prose keep "station"; where a task quotes on-screen
  text that says "station" for the Core, write "Core".
* **Attribution.** New NereusSDR-original C++ files start with
  `// no-port-check: NereusSDR-original.` Logic ported from Thetis (Task 33's `StopAllTx`
  from `console.cs`, Task 38's time-out from `TimeOutTimerManager.cs`, the display
  computations Task 20 moves, the Setup text and values Tasks 43 to 46 carry) follows
  `docs/attribution/HOW-TO-PORT.md`: the pre-port checklist, each source file's header
  byte for byte (Samphire's dual-licensing statement where the source has it), a
  `docs/attribution/THETIS-PROVENANCE.md` row, inline cites stamped `[v2.10.3.15]`
  (Thetis at `3759d096`), and every author tag preserved. Vendored or fetched libraries
  get a `docs/attribution/<NAME>-PROVENANCE.md` file with the pinned revision, archive
  hash and licence, and their licence text in `packaging/third-party-licenses/`.
* **The app's licence (D4, R-IOS-29).** Everything under `ios/` is NereusSDR-original,
  licensed GPLv3 plus the App Store permission in `ios/LICENSE` (Task 5). No code, no
  data tables and no text from Thetis, WDSP or AetherSDR go into `ios/`. Anything the
  desktop computes with ported code is computed by the station and reaches the phone as
  data; the phone displays station-supplied text (labels, tooltips) at run time but
  contains none. Third-party code bundled in the app is BSD, MIT, ISC, Apache-2.0 or
  MPL-2.0, with its notice on the licences screen. Conformance fixtures stay in
  `tests/data/link/` and are never bundled into the app; app test fixtures that stand in
  for Setup descriptions are synthetic.
* **Swift code** uses the Swift 6 language mode with strict concurrency, one primary type
  per file, and swift-testing (`import Testing`) for every test. Each Swift file starts
  with the two-line header of Task 5.
* **Commits:** GPG-signed, never `--no-gpg-sign`; no `Co-Authored-By` trailer; no em-dash
  (U+2014) in commit messages, documents or PR text. Stage explicit paths only.
* **Station tests** are built per target and run offscreen, never on screen:
  `cmake --build build --target tst_<name> && QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir build -R '^tst_<name>$' --output-on-failure`.
  The build directory is configured once with
  `cmake -S . -B build -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=RelWithDebInfo -DNEREUS_BUILD_TESTS=ON`.
  Read `docs/development/fast-test-loop.md` before any broad run; never run the unfiltered
  suite while iterating. New tests register with `nereus_add_test(tst_<name> …)` in
  `tests/CMakeLists.txt`; session tests add `fakes/LoopbackTransport.cpp`. Timing uses
  injected clocks, never sleeps.
* **App tests** run with `ios/scripts/swift-test.sh` (Task 5), which adds the
  command-line-tools flags when `xcode-select -p` points at the command line tools; the
  cross-implementation tests run with `ios/scripts/interop-test.sh` (Task 10). From Task 51
  on, app targets also build and test with
  `xcodebuild -project ios/NereusSDR.xcodeproj -scheme NereusSDR -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 17' test`
  after `ios/scripts/generate-project.sh`.
* **Secrets** never appear in the plan, the source, test fixtures, logs or support
  bundles: no tokens, private keys, pairing codes or device keys. Keys and codes in tests
  are generated at run time. A pairing code appears only on the Core's console, on its
  status page (served only to peers on directly connected networks, and only while the
  Core is unclaimed), on the Remote Access page of a desktop running the Core, and over
  the link only to sessions authenticated with a paired device's key (pairing design
  §4.3 and §4.5; spec §5.3 items 10 and 11). It never goes to a session signed in with
  the older access token, never into a log line or through the logging categories, and
  never literally into a fixture (write `"$string"`).
* **Devices** (the radios, the station computers, iPhones and iPads, the website's server)
  are touched only by the controller or JJ, never by an implementer on its own
  initiative. Confirm which device answers before writing to it; never restart or reflash
  a box that could drop off its network without asking. Hardware and device evidence
  stays **pending** until a device shows it.

## Decisions settled in this plan

Each follows from the spec, the designs or the code; none reopens a decision of JJ's.

* **The station listens on TCP 47910 on every interface by default** once identity
  exists (Task 12), beside the existing UDP 47910 announcement; the status page is TCP
  47911 (Task 17). Pairing is what makes the listener safe to expose, and the pairing
  design's first-run workflow (§11) needs it reachable without configuration.
  `remote_port = 0` still turns it off.
* **Device keys are ECDSA P-256** on every client (the phone's lives in the Secure
  Enclave); the station's identity key is P-256 too, and it signs the station's TLS
  certificate so a replaced certificate never breaks a saved station. An upgraded
  station stays claimed through its existing token: each window that signs in with it
  enrols its own key, and the token is retired by an explicit command; a new station
  creates no token (Tasks 12, 13).
* **Remote transmit is allowed for paired devices by default** (`remote_transmit = allow`
  in `nereusd.conf`), since D5 puts transmit in the first release.
* **The transmit numbers:** keepalive 100 ms, link-loss deadline 400 ms, starvation
  250 ms, transmit buffer 60 ms target and 120 ms maximum, first reconnect 1000 ms; the
  per-mode starvation action of Part F, with TUNE and two-tone exempt. Keepalives travel
  on their own unreliable channel on remote paths, key and unkey events are sent three
  times under a keying epoch, and the uplink is rate-matched to the radio's clock
  (Tasks 35 to 37).
* **The time-out applies by device kind** (D29): phones and tablets use new station
  settings, on at 180 s; keying at the station and from computers uses Thetis's own
  settings, off by default. The whole Thetis Time Out Timers group is ported (MOX and
  ping), since a port takes the whole upstream function.
* **No path change while keyed** (Task 29): a path that fails while keyed is covered by
  the watchdog, whose deadline is shorter than the first reconnect.
* **The web-only relay floor is chosen by measurement** (Task 29) among the options the
  App Store app can use: a patched libjuice speaking TURN over TCP and TLS, a local shim
  carrying TURN/UDP to coturn's TLS listener, or our own relay over a WebSocket. libnice
  is excluded because it needs GLib, which is LGPL and cannot go into the App Store app.
  JJ chooses from the measured comparison.
* **The rendezvous service is a small Python service** (Task 26), simpler to deploy and to
  self-host than a Qt build; the C++ and Swift clients run the same conformance vectors.
* **The station's radio choice follows the rules JJ gave the Core/GUI session:** a radio
  chosen from an app first, then the configuration file, automatic only when exactly one
  radio is visible, otherwise the station waits; the desktop manages a remote Core on a
  This Core page (Task 25).
* **The desktop remote window gains microphone transmit** (Task 36), for parity with the
  phone, through the existing capture helper and the microphone chosen in Audio >
  Devices, with the lossless format when the link can carry it; TCI transmit and VOX
  work remotely too (Tasks 35, 36).
* **Spot sources run at the station** (Task 21); a desktop remote window still runs its
  own WSJT-X and SpotCollector listeners for programs on its own computer (remote design
  §6.4).
* **Setup reaches the phone as station-supplied descriptions** (Part H), with a desktop
  parity test and a station live-apply test for every station control described.
* **D41 and D42 apply throughout:** an item the desktop has not built is left out and
  appears once it exists, using the same single list of unbuilt features the desktop
  hides by (R-R3-49); VAX Audio and Antenna Setup are offered from the start; MIDI
  Mapping and Macro Buttons are not built.

## What already exists

All paths are on `codex/integrate-r2-main` (read at `4bb89b5d`).

* **The control session:** `src/core/session/` holds `SessionMessages.{h,cpp}` (the JSON
  kinds; protocol 1.11; `hello` decoding ignores unknown keys; `kSessionProtocolMinor`
  frozen at 11 with features gated by capability versions), `SessionTransport`,
  `StationServer` (greets first, the connect sequence, preemption, heartbeat; ends a
  connection on an undecodable message), `StationClient` (the desktop's client; ignores
  unknown kinds), `IStationLink`, `StationCapabilities` (including
  `notchControlVersion`, `audioProfileVersion`, `audioClockVersion`), `MirrorSchema`,
  `MirrorPolicy`, `MirrorEnumDomain`, `ObjectRegistry`, `SessionCommandDispatcher` (an
  if-chain of verbs; results can carry values), `StationTelemetry`,
  `StationLanAnnouncement`, `StationLanAnnouncer`, `StationLanDiscovery`,
  `StationLanCache`, `RemoteStationOptions`, `Ps3DisplayCodec`, `PureSignalSessionFacade`,
  `DspCommandValues`.
* **Settings over the link:** `src/core/settings/SettingsProxyServer` and `SettingsScope`
  (`classifySettingsKey`; the Core applies only the DSP Options receive keys live today,
  `RadioModel.cpp:17174-17226`).
* **Media:** `src/core/session/media/` holds `DisplayCodec` (NSDC v1), `OpusAudioCodec`
  (24000 wideband, 48000 fullband), `PcmAudioCodec` (lossless L16), `MediaPeer`,
  `LibDataChannelMediaTransport`, `AudioJitterBuffer`, `RemoteAudioContext`,
  `DaemonMediaController`; `cmake/NereusRemoteMedia.cmake` pins libdatachannel 0.24.5,
  libjuice, libsrtp, usrsctp, plog and nlohmann json by codeload archive and SHA-256;
  `third_party/rade/cmake/BuildOpus.cmake` pins Opus `940d4e5a`. The desktop's client side
  is `src/gui/RemoteMediaController`, `src/gui/RemoteTelemetryController` and
  `MainWindow::ensureRemoteSession`.
* **Security today:** `src/core/security/TokenStore` (one shared token, 5 failures then a
  60 s lockout) and `CertificateStore` (self-signed RSA-3072, pinned by the SHA-256 of its
  DER).
* **The daemon:** `src/server_main.cpp` (`--config`, `--profile`), `src/core/daemon/DaemonApp`
  (sets a blanket receive-only policy, `DaemonApp.cpp:136`), `DaemonConfig`,
  `DaemonTelemetryController`; `packaging/nereusd.conf.sample` (`remote_port = 0`,
  `remote_bind = 127.0.0.1`); `packaging/nereusd.service.in`.
* **Radio and transmit:** `src/models/RadioModel`, `SliceModel`, `TransmitModel`,
  `TunerModel`, `FilterPresetStore`, `BandPlanManager`; `src/core/MoxController`
  (`moxChanged(int, bool, bool)`, `rxReady()`), `TxSliceArbiter`, `TxInterlockPolicy`,
  `TxMicRouter`, `TxChannel`, `TxWorkerThread`, `RxChannel`, `WdspEngine`, `NbFamily`,
  `PureSignal`, `PsccPump`, `TwoToneController`, `TxAnalyzer`, `MicProfileManager`;
  `src/core/audio/CompositeTxMicRouter`; `src/core/meters/SliceMeterPump`;
  `src/gui/meters/MeterPoller`; `src/core/PttSource`, `PttMode`; `src/core/safety/`;
  `src/core/BoardCapabilities`, `SkuUiProfile`; `third_party/wdsp/src/dsplock.c` (fair
  turn-taking at the DSP lock). No DSP-control thread exists; Tasks 30 to 32 build it.
* **Accessories:** `src/core/PgxlConnection`, `TgxlConnection`, `Rf2ksConnection`.
* **Spots:** `src/core/DxClusterClient`, `WsjtxClient`, `SpotCollectorClient`,
  `PotaClient`, `FreeDVReporterClient`, `PskReporterClient`; `src/models/SpotModel`,
  `FreeDVStationModel`. Only the GUI starts them (`src/gui/MainWindow.cpp:834-840`).
* **Desktop setup:** `src/gui/SetupDialog` (12 categories, 59 pages), the page classes in
  `src/gui/setup/` (the Remote Access page's stub at `CatNetworkSetupPages.h:201-212`),
  `src/gui/spectrum/PeakBlobDetector` and `ActivePeakHoldTrace` (Thetis ports),
  `src/gui/applets/VaxApplet`.
* **Remote-window pieces from the R3 lanes:** `src/gui/CoreTargetStore` (saved Cores,
  `ConnectionTargets/V1`), `src/gui/StationLanSelection`, `src/gui/ConnectionSelector`,
  `SetupScope` in `src/gui/SetupDialog.h` (Core or this computer, per page),
  `src/core/audio/CaptureSupervisor` (the capture helper, local sessions only today),
  `src/core/session/media/RemoteAudioRateMatcher`, and on `codex/lane-b` the remote
  window's own TCI server (R-R3-42, `c5ac13cc`).
* **Test fakes and helpers:** `tests/fakes/LoopbackTransport`, `LoopbackStationLink`,
  `ConnectableRadioModel`, `P1FakeRadio` and the P2 fake; `tests/OperatorWording.h`; the
  golden-and-regenerate precedent `tst_tci_init_burst_regen`; `NEREUS_TEST_DATA_DIR`.
  Test binaries land in `build/tests/`.

## Other plans this one relies on

These are owned by other plans on `codex/integrate-r2-main` (the other agent's lanes),
so this plan does not build them twice. Each task that needs one starts with a
"Before this task" check: the controller confirms the named tasks have merged into
`codex/integrate-r2-main` and merges that branch into `claude/iphone-app`; if they have
not, the controller stops and asks JJ.

* `2026-09-23-r3-core-owned-accessories-plan.md` Tasks 1 to 4 (the station's accessory
  controllers, the `amplifier`, `rfkit` and `tuner` objects, the live interlock command,
  the station's own TCI server, the accessory control document): needed by Tasks 25, 42,
  45 and 60.
* `2026-09-23-r3-remote-radio-hardware-plan.md` Tasks 1, 2 and 4 (the Core's radio
  identity, the `stepAtt` object, Hardware Config's receive settings through the Core with
  a live apply step and the Alex receive object): needed by Tasks 45 and 57.
* `2026-09-23-r3-unfinished-controls-plan.md` (R-R3-49): its single list of unbuilt
  features decides what the phone offers (Tasks 25 and 43), so both hide the same
  things. The R3 plan's "Built after R4" list names the new features JJ scheduled after
  R5 and R4 (receive EQ, SAM options and DC block, meter options, the logging page, local
  connection history and network diagnostics, spot filters, the RBN limit, muting VAX
  during transmit, RADE callsigns with the end-of-over tail, FreeDV to PSK Reporter, TCI
  settings); this plan builds none of them and leaves room for the RADE tail (Tasks 33,
  35, 37, 39, 54).
* The Core/GUI session's R5 and R4 scouting of 2026-09-23 (its notes
  `r5-scout-identity-pairing`, `r5-scout-transport`, `r5-scout-rendezvous-relay`,
  `r4-core-tx-scout` and `r4-gui-protocol-scout` in `~/.config/nereus/work/`) is folded
  into Parts C, E and F; implementers of those tasks read the matching note.
* The Thetis step-list port a separate session is doing in `SliceModel` (PR #325): the
  catalogue (Task 19) carries whatever list `SliceModel` holds, so nothing waits on it.

**Order and parts.** A (Tasks 1 to 4, station): the written link. B (5 to 11, phone):
the app's foundation. C (12 to 18): identity and pairing (15 and 16a phone, the rest

# Part A: The written link

The phone is the first client not built from this C++ tree, so the link is written
down and pinned by a conformance suite both ends run before anything else builds on it
(spec §4.3, §8 "Before any code"). Every later task that touches the wire updates the
surface manifest, the document and the fixtures in the same commit.

## Task 1: The link's surface, captured and guarded

**Runs in:** the Core/GUI session's lanes (a station task).

**Requirements:** R-IOS-01 (the surface half: what the link carries, captured from the
code so the written specification and the fixtures cannot drift from it).

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/core/session/SessionMessages.{h,cpp}` (add `SessionMessages::allKinds()`)
- Modify: `src/core/session/SessionCommandDispatcher.{h,cpp}` (add the declared verb table
  `SessionCommandDispatcher::verbSpecs()`; routing is unchanged)
- Create: `tests/LinkSurface.{h,cpp}` (builds the surface description from the code)
- Create: `tests/tst_link_surface_manifest.cpp` (compares the live surface with the
  committed file), `tests/tst_link_surface_manifest_regen.cpp` (writes it into the
  directory named by the environment variable `NEREUS_LINK_REGEN_OUT` when set, and
  otherwise into a temporary directory, so ctest can run it without touching the tree;
  the precedent is `tst_tci_init_burst_regen`)
- Create: `tests/data/link/v1/surface.json`
- Modify: `tests/CMakeLists.txt`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing.
- Produces:
  - `static QList<SessionMessageKind> SessionMessages::allKinds();` every enumerator in
    declaration order.
  - `struct CommandArgumentSpec { QByteArray name; MirrorWireKind kind; bool optional; };`
    `struct CommandVerbSpec { QByteArray verb; QList<CommandArgumentSpec> arguments; QByteArray capability; int capabilityVersion; quint16 minMinor; };`
    `static const QList<CommandVerbSpec>& SessionCommandDispatcher::verbSpecs();`
    A verb family routed by prefix (`ps3.`, `dspAssets.`, `notch.`) lists each concrete
    verb it accepts.
  - `QJsonObject LinkSurface::capture();` returning the surface object described below.
  - The file `tests/data/link/v1/surface.json`, whose top-level keys are exactly
    `messageKinds`, `capabilities`, `mirrorClasses`, `objectKeys`, `commands`,
    `settingsScope`, `telemetry`, `mediaControl`, `limits`.

**Acceptance:**
- `messageKinds` lists every `SessionMessageKind` by wire name with the keys a
  well-formed message of that kind must carry (the presence checks in
  `SessionMessages::decode`), e.g. `{"hello": {"required": ["type","major","minor","settingsSchema","peer"]}}`.
- `capabilities` lists every name `StationCapabilities::toUpdates()` emits for a fully
  populated descriptor, with its wire kind.
- `mirrorClasses` lists every class `MirrorSchema` registers, each property with its
  wire kind, its direction from `MirrorPolicy` (outbound only, bidirectional) and, for
  enums, the value list from `MirrorEnumDomain`. `objectKeys` lists the object key
  patterns (`radio`, `transmit`, `tuner`, `pan:<i>`, `slice:<id>`, and the rest the
  station registers at BASE).
- `commands` is `verbSpecs()` rendered as JSON.
- `settingsScope` lists the key prefixes `SettingsScope` classifies as Station.
- `telemetry` lists the field paths of `station.metrics.v1` at each
  `stationTelemetryVersion` the station emits.
- `mediaControl` lists each operation name in each direction with its exact field set.
- `limits` carries the numeric limits with their source constants: inbound caps (station
  1 MiB, client 8 MiB, media control 128 KiB, telemetry 16 KiB), heartbeat interval
  20000 ms and missed-pong count 2, connect deadline 30000 ms, delta flush 50 ms, client
  backoff `[1000,2000,5000,10000,30000,60000]` ms, maximum peers 8, maximum display
  endpoints 8, endpoint width 1 to 4096 pixels, frame rate 1 to 60.
- `tst_link_surface_manifest` passes on the unmodified tree and fails, naming the
  difference, when (checked by the test itself on a copy of the captured object) a
  capability is added, a mirrored property changes kind, or a verb gains an argument.
- A source scan in the test finds every `commandVerb == "<verb>"` and
  `commandVerb.startsWith("<prefix>")` literal in `SessionCommandDispatcher.cpp` and
  fails if a routed verb is missing from `verbSpecs()` or a listed verb is not routed.
- For every verb in `verbSpecs()`, dispatching it with its declared argument shape on a
  `ConnectableRadioModel` never yields the dispatcher's unknown-verb refusal, and an
  unlisted verb always does.

**Verification:** ordinary feature (test-first for the drift guard). Unit, offscreen:
`cmake --build build --target tst_link_surface_manifest && QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir build -R '^tst_link_surface_manifest$' --output-on-failure`.
Regenerate with `cmake --build build --target tst_link_surface_manifest_regen && NEREUS_LINK_REGEN_OUT=tests/data/link/v1 QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ./build/tests/tst_link_surface_manifest_regen`
and review the diff before committing it; the file is never accepted unread.

**Execution note (advisory):** opus. No networking, no device. First task; nothing
before it.

- [ ] **Step 1:** Add `allKinds()` and `verbSpecs()` with the source-scan and routing
      tests; routing code unchanged.
- [ ] **Step 2:** Write `LinkSurface::capture()` and the regen target; generate
      `surface.json`, read it whole, commit it with the guard test.

## Task 2: The link specification

**Runs in:** the Core/GUI session's lanes (a station task).

**Requirements:** R-IOS-01 (the written specification: framing, handshake, snapshot,
messages, capabilities, version rules).

**Files:**
- Create: `docs/architecture/2026-09-23-station-link-v1.md`
- Create: `scripts/render-link-tables.py` (renders the tables between
  `<!-- surface:<section> -->` and `<!-- /surface -->` markers from `surface.json`;
  `--check` exits 1 when the document is out of date)
- Modify: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (a step in the `compliance` job runs
  `python3 scripts/render-link-tables.py --check`)
- Modify: `CLAUDE.md` (a row for the document in the Documentation Index)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `tests/data/link/v1/surface.json` (Task 1).
- Produces: the document every later wire task updates; its section numbering is
  stable from here on (later tasks add sections, never renumber).

**Acceptance:**
- The document is the index of the whole link and names its normative parts, each
  still maintained in its own file: `2026-09-20-remote-media-control-v1.md`,
  `2026-09-20-display-codec-v1.md`, `2026-09-23-remote-notch-control-v1.md`, and
  `2026-09-23-remote-accessory-control-v1.md` once the accessories plan writes it.
- It states, in prose with exact values: transport (TLS 1.2 or later, WebSocket text
  frames, one JSON object per frame with a `type` key); the certificate (self-signed
  RSA-3072, SHA-256 signature, 10 years) and its pin (SHA-256 of the whole DER
  certificate, uppercase hex pairs joined by colons, 95 characters); that the station
  sends `hello` first; the connect sequence (hello and `auth.request`, then
  `auth.result`, `capabilities`, `settings.snapshot`, one `schema` per class, one
  `object.create` per object, `snapshot.complete`); the resend of `capabilities` and
  `settings.snapshot` on a late radio or a changed display allowance; the version rule
  as built (refuse on a different major, agree the lower minor) and the two-key feature
  gate (agreed minor at least N **and** the feature's capability version at least 1);
  the per-feature capability versions the station now uses instead of new minors; the
  token (256-bit, base64url, 5 failures then a 60 s lockout); the heartbeat, deadlines,
  caps, preemption and `retryable` classes; how numbers that are not finite travel
  (`"nan"`, `"inf"`, `"-inf"` strings); property writes and `property.result`; the
  settings proxy (station-scoped prefixes, origin tags, write-through); commands and
  results (argument kinds, `affectedKeys`, result values); telemetry; the LAN
  announcement (UDP 47910, 239.255.42.99 and ff12::4e52:5344, hop limit 1, every
  5 s, at most 512 bytes, schema 1 fields); what an older client does with an unknown
  message kind (logs and ignores it) and what the station does with one (ends the
  connection, not retryable), which is why a client never sends a kind or verb the
  station has not advertised.
- Every table listing kinds, capabilities, classes, properties, verbs, scope prefixes,
  telemetry fields, media operations and limits is generated by
  `render-link-tables.py`; `--check` passes on the committed document and fails after
  any edit to `surface.json`.
- A section "Conformance" describes the fixture format Task 3 defines, and a section
  "Changing the link" states the rule in Global Constraints.
- The document passes the repository's documentation lint and contains no em-dash.

**Verification:** documentation with generated content. `python3 scripts/render-link-tables.py --check`
exits 0; a deliberate one-line edit to a copy of `surface.json` in the scratchpad makes
`--check --surface <copy>` exit 1. Read-through against the code for every prose value.

**Execution note (advisory):** opus: each prose value is a claim about code that the
conformance suite will pin, so it is read from the source, not summarised. Requires
Task 1.

- [ ] **Step 1:** Write the renderer with `--check` and `--surface`.
- [ ] **Step 2:** Write the document; render its tables; add the CI check and the
      `CLAUDE.md` row.

## Task 3: The conformance suite on the station

**Runs in:** the Core/GUI session's lanes (a station task).

**Requirements:** R-IOS-01 (the conformance suite the station and the app both run).

**Files:**
- Create: `tests/data/link/v1/manifest.json`, `tests/data/link/v1/control/*.json`,
  `tests/data/link/v1/sessions/*.json`, `tests/data/link/v1/media/*.bin` with
  `*.expect.json`
- Create: `tests/LinkFixtures.{h,cpp}` (loader, placeholder matcher, script player)
- Create: `tests/tst_link_conformance_control.cpp`, `tests/tst_link_conformance_session.cpp`,
  `tests/tst_link_conformance_media.cpp`, `tests/tst_link_conformance_regen.cpp` (writes
  the media vectors; built on demand)
- Modify: `tests/CMakeLists.txt`, `docs/architecture/2026-09-23-station-link-v1.md`
  (the Conformance section)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `surface.json` and the document (Tasks 1, 2).
- Produces the fixture format both runners implement:
  - `manifest.json`: `{"linkMajors":[1],"fixtures":[{"id":"<fixture id>","file":"<path under v1/>","kind":"control"|"session"|"media","requires":{"<feature>":<version>}}]}`;
    `requires` is `{}` for fixtures every major-1 station passes.
  - A control fixture: `{"from":"station"|"client","wire":{<the exact message>},"decodes":true|false}`.
    Each end decodes `wire`; when `decodes` is true it re-encodes and the two JSON
    objects compare equal after parsing (key order ignored).
  - A session fixture: `{"stationSetup":{<the fake radio model and settings>},"steps":[<steps>]}`
    where a step is `{"from":"station"|"client","message":{<a message, placeholders allowed>}}`,
    `{"advanceMs":N}` or
    `{"expectClosed":{"retryable":true|false}}`. The station runner injects the client's
    messages and checks the station's; the app runner does the reverse.
  - Placeholders inside expected messages: `"$any"`, `"$string"`, `"$int"`,
    `"$capture:<name>"` (records the value) and `"$ref:<name>"` (must equal it).
  - A media fixture: the encoded bytes plus `{"codec":"nsdc1"|"ps3d"|"opus","expect":{<decoded values>}}`
    with the decoded values and a tolerance (display bins within 0.01 dB; Opus PCM
    within 2 least significant bits of the reference or at least 60 dB SNR, whichever
    the fixture states).
  - `LinkFixtures::runSession(const QJsonObject& fixture, StationServer& server, LoopbackTransport& transport)`
    returning a `QString` failure description, empty on pass.

**Acceptance:**
- Control fixtures cover every message kind at least once in each direction it
  travels, plus negative cases: a frame over each cap, a missing required key, a wrong
  type, a `hello` major out of range, an unknown `type`.
- Session fixtures cover: the full connect sequence to `snapshot.complete` on a
  `ConnectableRadioModel` with one slice; a wrong token; five wrong tokens then the
  lockout; a different major refused; a lower minor agreed; a second authenticated
  connection preempting the first (the first gets `session.end`, `retryable:false`);
  ping and pong; the connect deadline (`advanceMs` 30000 without auth closes); a
  property write and its `property.result`; a settings write and its echo; each verb in
  `verbSpecs()` with valid arguments and one with a wrong argument name.
- Media fixtures: at least three NSDC v1 frames (full, delta, keyframe after loss) and
  one PS3D frame with decoded values; four Opus packets at the station's settings
  (48 kHz stereo, 1920-sample frames, 24000 bit/s, wideband) with reference PCM; the
  vectors are written by `tst_link_conformance_regen` from the station's own encoders.
- All three conformance tests pass on the station; each fails with a readable message
  when its fixture is altered (checked inside the test on an in-memory copy).
- Timing uses `advanceMs` through injected clocks; no test sleeps.

**Verification:** ordinary feature. Unit, offscreen:
`cmake --build build --target tst_link_conformance_control tst_link_conformance_session tst_link_conformance_media && QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir build -R '^tst_link_conformance_(control|session|media)$' --output-on-failure`.

**Execution note (advisory):** opus. Requires Tasks 1 and 2. Session work touches
authentication behaviour only in tests; no production change.

- [ ] **Step 1:** The format, loader and matcher, with the control fixtures.
- [ ] **Step 2:** The session player and fixtures; the media vectors and their runner;
      the document's Conformance section.

## Task 4: Versions both ways, and declared features

**Runs in:** the Core/GUI session's lanes (a station task).

**Requirements:** R-IOS-01 (version rules), D23 and D39 (§4.4: each end supports its
own major and the one before it and agrees the highest shared; only two or more majors
apart is refused, naming both), and the feature declaration the later tasks need
before capabilities arrive.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/core/session/SessionMessages.{h,cpp}` (`hello` gains `majors` and
  `features`)
- Modify: `src/core/session/StationServer.{h,cpp}`, `src/core/session/StationClient.{h,cpp}`
- Create: `src/core/session/LinkVersion.{h,cpp}`
- Modify: `tests/data/link/v1/` (hello fixtures and a negotiation session per case),
  `surface.json`, the link document (a Versions section)
- Test: `tests/tst_link_version.cpp`, the conformance runners

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the suite (Task 3).
- Produces:
  - `inline constexpr std::array<quint16, 1> kSupportedSessionMajors{1};` in
    `LinkVersion.h` (the station's and the desktop client's supported majors).
  - `std::optional<quint16> LinkVersion::agreeMajor(QList<quint16> ours, QList<quint16> theirs);`
    the highest major in both lists, or nullopt.
  - `QString LinkVersion::refusalText(QList<quint16> station, QList<quint16> client);`
  - `hello` fields `majors` (array of whole numbers) and `features` (object, feature
    name to whole-number version). Both optional on decode: absent `majors` means
    `[major]`, absent `features` means `{}`. Unknown feature names are ignored.
  - `SessionMessage::supportedMajors` (`QList<quint16>`) and
    `SessionMessage::features` (`QHash<QByteArray,int>`).
  - `bool StationServer::peerDeclares(SessionTransport* peer, const QByteArray& feature, int minVersion) const;`
  - `bool StationClient::stationDeclares(const QByteArray& feature, int minVersion) const;`

**Acceptance:**
- `agreeMajor({1},{1})` is 1; `agreeMajor({1,2},{2,3})` is 2; `agreeMajor({2,3},{1})`
  is nullopt; `agreeMajor({1},{2,3})` is nullopt; `agreeMajor({3,4},{2,3})` is 3.
- The station sends `majors` and `features` in its `hello`; a client `hello` whose
  `major` is in the station's list is accepted and the session runs at that major; any
  other is refused with `session.end`, `retryable:false`, and the reason
  `refusalText(stationMajors, clientMajors)`, which names both sides' versions in plain words and passes
  `OperatorWording::isPlain`, e.g. "This Core runs link version 1 and this app runs
  version 3. Update the Core."
- The desktop client picks the highest shared major from the station's `hello` and
  sends it; with no shared major it disconnects without retrying and shows the
  station's-side wording.
- A peer that sends no `majors` or `features` behaves exactly as today (the existing
  session fixtures pass unchanged except the new keys in the station's `hello`).
- Every gate already built sends older peers exactly what it sends today: the agreed-minor
  gates (including the radio identity entries sent only at minor 11) and the capability
  versions (`receiverAudioVersion`, `radioHardwareVersion` and the rest); the surface
  manifest and the existing goldens pin it.
- Negotiation is tested with injected supported lists (a test-only constructor
  argument), because a second major does not exist yet; the conformance manifest's
  `linkMajors` is `[1]` and each runner loops over it.
- Debug builds of `nereusd` accept `--test-link-majors <list>` (for example `1,2`), which
  replaces the advertised list, so the app's version screens can be exercised against a
  real station (R-IOS-16's integration evidence); release builds reject the option.

**Verification:** authorisation and connection behaviour, so invariant coverage first:
write the negotiation table test before changing the servers. Unit and conformance,
offscreen:
`cmake --build build --target tst_link_version tst_link_conformance_session tst_station_session && QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen ctest --test-dir build -R '^(tst_link_version|tst_link_conformance_session|tst_station_session)$' --output-on-failure`.

**Execution note (advisory):** opus. Touches the connect path (flag for earlier review
with the other authorisation tasks). Requires Task 3.

- [ ] **Step 1:** `LinkVersion` and its table test; `hello` encode and decode with the
      lenient defaults.
- [ ] **Step 2:** Station and desktop client negotiation, feature declaration queries,
      fixtures, surface and document.

# Part B: The app's foundation (no Xcode needed)

Everything here is a Swift package, `NereusKit`, that builds and tests on macOS with
the command line tools, so the app's link, state, media and band logic are proven
against the conformance suite long before the app has a screen.

## Task 5: The app's tree, licence and provenance check

**Runs in:** the phone session, on `claude/iphone-app` (a phone task).

**Requirements:** R-IOS-29 (the licence and a provenance check over the app's sources
in CI), D4, spec §4.11.

**Files:**
- Create: `ios/LICENSE` (the full GNU GPL version 3 text, followed by the additional
  permission below)
- Create: `ios/README.md` (what the app is, the module map, how to test)
- Create: `ios/THIRD-PARTY.md` (one row per vendored library: name, version or commit,
  archive URL, archive SHA-256, licence, path under `ios/`; empty table now)
- Create: `ios/NereusKit/Package.swift`, `ios/NereusKit/Sources/NereusModels/Frequency.swift`,
  `ios/NereusKit/Tests/NereusModelsTests/FrequencyTests.swift`
- Create: `ios/scripts/swift-test.sh`
- Create: `scripts/verify-ios-provenance.py`, `tests/compliance/test_verify_ios_provenance.py`
  (where the other checkers' tests live; CI's `compliance` job runs that folder with pytest)
- Create: `.github/workflows/ios.yml` (the `package` job)
- Modify: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (a `compliance` step runs
  `python3 scripts/verify-ios-provenance.py`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing.
- Produces:
  - The package: `swift-tools-version:6.0`; `platforms: [.iOS("17.4"), .macOS(.v14)]`;
    Swift 6 language mode; library products are added per module by later tasks.
  - `NereusModels.Frequency`, a value type holding whole hertz as `Int64`, with
    `init(hz:)`, `var hz`, `var megahertzText: String` (`"14.074000"`), and `Comparable`.
  - `ios/scripts/swift-test.sh [swift test arguments]`: runs `swift test` in
    `ios/NereusKit`; when `xcode-select -p` prints the command line tools path it adds
    `-Xswiftc -F -Xswiftc "$FW" -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker "$FW" -Xlinker -rpath -Xlinker "$LIB"`
    with `FW=/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/Frameworks` and
    `LIB=/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools/Library/Developer/usr/lib`; exits with
    `swift test`'s status.
  - The licence header every app source file starts with:
    ```swift
    // NereusSDR for iOS: <what this file is for>
    // SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-or-later WITH AdditionRef-NereusSDR-AppStore-permission
    ```
  - The additional permission, appended to `ios/LICENSE` verbatim:
    ```text
    Additional permission under GNU GPL version 3 section 7

    The copyright holders of NereusSDR for iOS give you permission to convey
    this program, and works based on it, through Apple's App Store and
    TestFlight under the terms Apple imposes on software distributed there
    (including the Apple Media Services Terms and Conditions and the App Store
    Usage Rules), even where those terms add restrictions that section 10 of
    the GNU General Public License would otherwise forbid, provided that the
    complete Corresponding Source of the version you convey is available to
    every recipient, free of charge, under the GNU General Public License
    version 3 or later. This permission covers only code whose copyright
    holders have granted it. You may remove this permission from copies you
    convey; if you do, the rest of the license applies unchanged.
    ```

**Acceptance:**
- `verify-ios-provenance.py` exits 1, naming the file and the rule, when any of these
  holds, and 0 otherwise:
  - a `.swift`, `.c`, `.h`, `.m`, `.metal` or `.cpp` file under `ios/` outside a
    directory listed in `ios/THIRD-PARTY.md` lacks the two header lines;
  - such a file contains `Thetis`, `WDSP`, `wdsp`, `AetherSDR`, `FlexRadio`, `NR0V`,
    `MW0LGE`, `Warren Pratt`, or a `Copyright (C)` line;
  - a vendored directory's licence is not BSD-2-Clause, BSD-3-Clause, MIT, ISC,
    Apache-2.0 or MPL-2.0, or its row lacks the archive SHA-256;
  - any file under `ios/` other than tests refers to `tests/data/link` (fixtures are
    never bundled into the app).
- The Python test feeds the checker a temporary tree for each rule and asserts the exit
  code and message.
- `ios/scripts/swift-test.sh` passes on this Mac with the command line tools only
  (`FrequencyTests`: `Frequency(hz: 14_074_000).megahertzText == "14.074000"`, ordering,
  and `hz` round trip).
- The `ios.yml` `package` job runs on `macos-26` on pushes and pull requests touching
  `ios/**`, `tests/data/link/**` or the workflow, and runs `ios/scripts/swift-test.sh`.

**Verification:** documentation plus a checker with its own tests.
`python3 -m pytest tests/compliance/test_verify_ios_provenance.py -q` passes (the checker
and its test run on this Mac's Python 3.9 as well as CI's 3.12);
`python3 scripts/verify-ios-provenance.py` exits 0 on the tree;
`ios/scripts/swift-test.sh` passes.

**Execution note (advisory):** opus. No networking, no device. Requires nothing; can
run in a worktree in parallel with Part A.

## Task 6: Opus in the app

**Runs in:** the phone session, on `claude/iphone-app` (a phone task).

**Requirements:** spec §4.1 (audio playback on the phone; the microphone's Opus
uplink), R-IOS-01 (the app runs the Opus conformance vectors).

**Files:**
- Create: `ios/scripts/vendor-sources.sh` (downloads a named library's pinned archive,
  checks its SHA-256, extracts the listed files into `ios/NereusKit/Sources/<target>/`,
  and prints the `THIRD-PARTY.md` row)
- Create: `ios/NereusKit/Sources/COpus/` (vendored), `ios/NereusKit/Sources/COpusShim/`
  (`include/COpusShim.h`, `COpusShim.c`)
- Create: `ios/NereusKit/Sources/NereusMedia/OpusDecoder.swift`, `OpusEncoder.swift`
- Create: `ios/NereusKit/Tests/NereusMediaTests/OpusTests.swift`,
  `ios/NereusKit/Tests/NereusMediaTests/LinkFixtureLoader.swift`
- Modify: `ios/NereusKit/Package.swift`, `ios/THIRD-PARTY.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the conformance media fixtures (Task 3).
- Produces:
  - Opus from `https://github.com/xiph/opus/archive/940d4e5af64351ca8ba8390df3f555484c567fbb.zip`
    (the station's pin in `third_party/rade/cmake/BuildOpus.cmake`), float build,
    `dnn/` excluded, defines `OPUS_BUILD`, `USE_ALLOCA`, `HAVE_LRINTF`, `HAVE_LRINT`,
    `PACKAGE_VERSION="940d4e5a"`.
  - `COpusShim` non-variadic wrappers: `nereus_opus_set_bitrate`,
    `nereus_opus_set_bandwidth`, `nereus_opus_set_complexity`, `nereus_opus_set_vbr`,
    `nereus_opus_set_vbr_constraint`, `nereus_opus_set_inband_fec`,
    `nereus_opus_set_packet_loss_perc`, `nereus_opus_set_dtx`, `nereus_opus_set_signal`,
    `nereus_opus_get_bitrate`.
  - `final class OpusDecoder { init(channels: Int) throws; func decode(_ packet: Data) throws -> [Float]; func concealLoss(frames: Int) throws -> [Float] }`
    at 48000 Hz, interleaved output.
  - `final class OpusEncoder { init(profile: OpusEncoder.Profile) throws; func encode(_ pcm: [Float]) throws -> Data }`
    with `Profile.microphone`: mono, 48000 Hz, 960-sample (20 ms) frames, application
    VOIP, 24000 bit/s constrained VBR, in-band FEC on, expected loss 10 %, DTX off,
    complexity 9, signal voice.
  - `LinkFixtureLoader.root`: the repository's `tests/data/link/v1` found from
    `#filePath`, used by every app conformance test.

**Acceptance:**
- Each Opus media fixture the app runs decodes within its stated tolerance of the
  station's reference PCM, 1920 stereo samples per packet. Which fixtures the app runs,
  and how a fixture that decodes after others on one decoder is played, come from the
  link document's Conformance section.
- `concealLoss(frames: 1920)` after a decoded packet returns 3840 finite floats.
- A 1 kHz sine at -12 dBFS encoded with `.microphone` and decoded has an SNR of at
  least 20 dB against the input after aligning for the codec's delay; every packet is at
  most 1000 bytes; 50 packets per second of audio.
- `vendor-sources.sh opus` reproduces the committed files byte for byte (the test script
  runs it into a temporary directory and compares).

**Verification:** unit. `ios/scripts/swift-test.sh --filter OpusTests`; then
`python3 scripts/verify-ios-provenance.py`.

**Execution note (advisory):** opus. Requires Tasks 3 and 5.

## Task 7: Display frames in the app

**Runs in:** the phone session, on `claude/iphone-app` (a phone task).

**Requirements:** spec §4.1 (the station sends display frames per endpoint; the phone
draws them), R-IOS-01.

**Files:**
- Create: `ios/NereusKit/Sources/NereusMedia/DisplayFrameDecoder.swift`,
  `DisplayFrame.swift`
- Create: `ios/NereusKit/Tests/NereusMediaTests/DisplayFrameDecoderTests.swift`
- Modify: `ios/NereusKit/Package.swift`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `docs/architecture/2026-09-20-display-codec-v1.md` (the NSDC v1 format,
  NereusSDR-original) and the NSDC media fixtures (Task 3). PS3D fixtures are not run by
  the app: the phone shows PureSignal as on, off and status only (spec §5.2 item 3).
- Produces:
  - `struct DisplayFrame { let endpointId: UInt32; let contextGeneration: UInt32; let encoderSequence: UInt32; let producerTimestamp: UInt64 /* sender-monotonic ns */; let isKeyframe: Bool; let waterfallAdvance: Bool; let minDbm: Float; let maxDbm: Float; let traceDbm: [Float]; let waterfallDbm: [Float]; let wideDbm: [Float] /* empty without a wide row */ }`:
    the NSDC v1 header and its trace, waterfall and optional wide planes. The frequency
    axis is not a codec field; it comes with the endpoint's context (Task 11).
  - `enum DisplayDecodeDisposition { case accepted, needKeyframe, rejected }` and
    `enum DisplayDecodeReason { case none, invalidInput, noHistory, sequenceGap, staleSequence, oldContext, contextMismatch, badMagic, unsupportedVersion, unknownFlags, truncated, oversized, malformed }`,
    the station's `DisplayCodecDisposition` and `DisplayCodecReason`
    (`src/core/session/media/DisplayCodec.h`), whose names the vectors carry.
  - `struct DisplayDecodeResult { let disposition: DisplayDecodeDisposition; let reason: DisplayDecodeReason; let frame: DisplayFrame? }`
    and `final class DisplayFrameDecoder { func decode(_ datagram: Data) -> DisplayDecodeResult; var needsKeyframe: Bool { get } }`,
    one per endpoint, with the document's "State and recovery" rules: a gap needs a
    keyframe and leaves the history untouched until one arrives; an older or equal
    sequence (the unsigned half-range rule) or an older context generation is refused; a
    newer generation needs a keyframe; a malformed packet is refused without changing the
    history.
  - Reconstruction exactly as the document and the station's decoder do it: a quantised
    value `q` in 0...255, absolute or the previous reconstructed plane's value plus a
    residual, maps to
    `Float(min(max(minDbm + Double(q) * (maxDbm - minDbm) / 255.0, minDbm), maxDbm))`.

**Acceptance:**
- Every `nsdc1` vector decodes to its expected disposition, reason, header fields and
  `traceDbm`, `waterfallDbm` and `wideDbm` rows within its `dbm` tolerance (0.01), played
  as the link document's section 16.4 says, `after` chains included.
- A delta whose predecessor was lost yields `needKeyframe` with `sequenceGap` and
  `needsKeyframe == true`; the following keyframe is accepted and clears it (the
  `nsdc1-delta-after-loss` and `nsdc1-keyframe-after-loss` vectors).
- A truncated datagram, a wrong magic, an unknown version and a row longer than 4096
  samples each come back `rejected` with the reason the station's decoder gives for the
  same bytes (`truncated`, `badMagic`, `unsupportedVersion`, and whichever of `malformed`
  or `oversized` `DisplayCodec.cpp` returns for the long row), leave the history
  untouched, and never crash.
- The decoder was written from the document, not translated from
  `src/core/session/media/DisplayCodec.cpp`; where the two disagree, the test follows
  the station's fixtures and the controller asks the Core/GUI session to correct the
  document.

**Verification:** unit. `ios/scripts/swift-test.sh --filter DisplayFrameDecoderTests`.

**Execution note (advisory):** opus. Requires Tasks 3 and 5; can run beside Task 6.

- [ ] **Step 1:** Header parsing and reconstruction with the fixture tests.
- [ ] **Step 2:** Loss handling and the malformed-input cases.

## Task 8: The control session in the app

**Runs in:** the phone session, on `claude/iphone-app` (a phone task).

**Requirements:** R-IOS-01 (the app side of the suite), R-IOS-16 (connecting, the
version rules of §4.4, a typed address), spec §7 (link lost: retry with backoff,
cancellable).

**Files:**
- Create: `ios/NereusKit/Sources/NereusLink/` with `StationEndpoint.swift`,
  `ManualAddress.swift`, `LinkMessage.swift`, `LinkCodec.swift`, `StationSession.swift`,
  `CertificatePin.swift`, `LinkVersionPolicy.swift`, `LinkFeatures.swift`,
  `ReconnectPolicy.swift`, `StationAuthenticator.swift`
- Create: `ios/NereusKit/Tests/NereusLinkTests/` with `ManualAddressTests.swift`,
  `LinkConformanceControlTests.swift`, `LinkConformanceSessionTests.swift`,
  `ReconnectPolicyTests.swift`, `StationSessionLoopbackTests.swift`
- Modify: `ios/NereusKit/Package.swift`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the control and session fixtures (Task 3), the version and feature fields
  (Task 4).
- Produces:
  - `struct StationEndpoint: Hashable, Codable { var host: String; var port: UInt16 }`
    with the default port 47910 (Task 12 makes the station listen there).
  - `enum ManualAddress { static func parse(_ text: String) -> StationEndpoint? }`
    accepting a hostname, an IPv4 literal, a bracketed IPv6 literal, each with an
    optional `:port`.
  - `enum LinkMessage` covering every message kind in `surface.json`, with
    `LinkCodec.decode(_ text: String) throws -> LinkMessage` and
    `LinkCodec.encode(_ message: LinkMessage) -> String`; numbers that are not finite
    travel as the strings `"nan"`, `"inf"` and `"-inf"`.
  - `protocol StationAuthenticator: Sendable { func authRequest(stationHello: LinkMessage.Hello, certificateSHA256: Data) async throws -> LinkMessage.AuthRequest }`;
    `TokenAuthenticator` (the legacy token; tests and older stations) is the only
    implementation here; Task 15 adds the device-key one.
  - `actor StationSession` with `init(endpoint:trust:authenticator:clock:)`,
    `func connect() async`, `func disconnect()`, `func send(_ message: LinkMessage) async throws`,
    `var events: AsyncStream<StationSession.Event>` where `Event` is
    `.stateChanged(State)`, `.message(LinkMessage)`, `.refused(Refusal)`; `State` is
    `.idle`, `.connecting`, `.authenticating`, `.receivingSnapshot`, `.ready`,
    `.waitingToRetry(seconds: Int)`, `.stopped`.
  - `enum StationTrust { case certificate(pinSHA256: Data); case identity(publicKey: Data) }`;
    only `.certificate` is used before Task 15.
  - `struct Refusal { enum Reason { case stationTooOld(station: [UInt16], app: [UInt16]); case appTooOld(station: [UInt16], app: [UInt16]); case authentication(String); case ended(String, retryable: Bool) } }`
  - `LinkVersionPolicy.supportedMajors = [1]`, and `agree(ours:theirs:) -> UInt16?`
    with the same table as the station's `LinkVersion::agreeMajor`; in debug builds the
    launch argument `-NereusLinkMajors 1,2` replaces the list, for the version screens'
    integration checks (Task 70), and release builds ignore it.
  - The app's `hello`: `major` is the highest major both ends support, read from the
    station's `majors` (a station's own `major` names only its oldest supported major,
    so it is used alone only when `majors` is absent); `majors` is
    `LinkVersionPolicy.supportedMajors`; `features` is `LinkFeatures.app`, a
    `static let [String: Int]` in `LinkFeatures.swift`, empty here. Each later task that
    builds something the station gates on a declared feature adds its entry:
    `deviceAuth` (Task 15), `remoteTx` (Task 55), `sessionHolder` (Task 56),
    `setupDescription` (Task 58). The name and meaning of `sessionHolder` follow the
    station's design for several devices (spec §3.9); the Core/GUI session tells the
    phone session before changing any of these four names.
  - The app's `hello` carries every field the link document's hello section requires of
    a client, with the values it gives: `major`, `minor` 11 (the agreed minor gates
    features, and the app-run fixtures require 11), `majors`, `features`, `peer` and
    `settingsSchema`. When no major is shared the session sends nothing after the
    station's `hello`: neither its own `hello` nor any credential.
  - `StationSession.send` refuses locally, sending nothing, whatever the link would end
    the session for: anything but `hello` and `auth.request` before sign-in, a second
    `hello`, an `auth.request` out of order, `media.control` before
    `snapshot.complete`, and any message over the station's 1 MiB cap. Messages go as
    compact JSON in text frames.
  - `LinkCodec` keeps the `media.control` payload as an opaque JSON object for Task 11,
    within the link document's decode caps (128 KiB for `media.control`, 16 KiB for
    telemetry).
  - An attempt whose connect sequence has not completed 30 s after the WebSocket opened
    ends as a retryable failure, matching the station's own deadline.
  - `ReconnectPolicy`: delays 1, 2, 5, 10, 30, 60 s then 60 s repeating; `reset()` on
    a session that reached `.ready`; `cancel()` stops the next attempt at once.

**Acceptance:**
- `ManualAddress.parse` accepts `shack.example.net`, `shack.example.net:5000`,
  `192.0.2.7`, `192.0.2.7:5000`, `[2001:db8::1]`, `[2001:db8::1]:5000`, returning port
  47910 where none is given; it rejects `2001:db8::1` without brackets, `host:0`,
  `host:65536`, an empty string and `http://host`.
- The control conformance test decodes and re-encodes every control fixture (each applies
  to both ends; only session fixtures carry `runs`), equal after parsing; negative
  fixtures throw.
- The session conformance test plays every session fixture the app runs, in the
  client's role, by the rules of the link document's Conformance section (the steps
  the runner scripts, the behaviour the app must produce, placeholders and
  tolerances), and passes them all, including the fixture that requires `majors` and
  `features` in the client's `hello`.
- The WebSocket accepts messages up to 8 MiB and answers the station's pings; the
  session sends its own ping every 20 s and treats two missed pongs as a lost link. The
  heartbeat is WebSocket ping and pong frames, never a message kind.
- The certificate check rejects any certificate whose DER SHA-256 differs from the pin,
  before any byte of `hello` is read.
- A station `hello` with no shared major yields `.refused(.stationTooOld)` or
  `.refused(.appTooOld)` by which side is behind, and no retry; nothing is sent after
  the station's `hello`.
- A refused sign-in (`auth.result` not accepted, then the connection closes with no
  `session.end`) yields `.refused(.authentication(reason))` with the station's words,
  and the session retries only when that `auth.result` says `retryable` (a lockout does,
  a wrong credential does not), so a stranger's failed attempts never lock the operator
  out for good.
- The session redials only after a lost link or a `session.end` whose `retryable` is
  true; a non-retryable end (preempted, an undecodable message) stops it, as the
  app-run `unknown-kind` fixture checks.
- `send` refuses each case listed under Produces locally and sends nothing; a message
  over 1 MiB never leaves; an attempt still short of `snapshot.complete` 30 s after the
  WebSocket opened ends and follows the retry schedule.
- A `session.end` that arrives before `hello` (a station already at its connection
  limit) yields `.refused(.ended(reason, retryable:))`, and whether the session retries
  follows `retryable`.
- A message kind the app does not know is logged and ignored, and the session carries
  on.
- After a lost link the session moves through `.waitingToRetry` on the policy's
  schedule, driven by an injected clock in the test; `disconnect()` during a wait stops
  it with no further attempt.
- `StationSessionLoopbackTests` runs the session against a TLS WebSocket echo station
  written in the test with Network framework on 127.0.0.1 and a generated certificate,
  and reaches `.ready` through the connect sequence.

**Verification:** authorisation and connection behaviour: conformance and loopback
tests first. `ios/scripts/swift-test.sh --filter NereusLinkTests`.

**Execution note (advisory):** opus. Networking (loopback only in tests). Requires
Tasks 3, 4 and 5.

- [ ] **Step 1:** Addresses, messages and codec with the control conformance test.
- [ ] **Step 2:** The session: TLS pin, WebSocket, hello and versions, authentication,
      heartbeat, reconnect; the session conformance and loopback tests.

## Task 9: The station's state in the app

**Runs in:** the phone session, on `claude/iphone-app` (a phone task).

**Requirements:** R-IOS-18 (each control writes to its owner), R-IOS-01, spec §4.2 R2
(the state mirror, the settings proxy).

**Files:**
- Create: `ios/NereusKit/Sources/NereusMirror/` with `MirrorStore.swift`,
  `MirrorValue.swift`, `MirrorObject.swift`, `SettingsProxyClient.swift`,
  `CommandClient.swift`, `StationTelemetryDecoder.swift`
- Create: `ios/NereusKit/Tests/NereusMirrorTests/` with `MirrorStoreTests.swift`,
  `SettingsProxyClientTests.swift`, `CommandClientTests.swift`,
  `MirrorConformanceTests.swift`
- Modify: `ios/NereusKit/Package.swift`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `StationSession` and `LinkMessage` (Task 8).
- Produces:
  - `enum MirrorValue: Equatable { case bool(Bool), int(Int64), double(Double), text(String), enumeration(Int64) }`:
    an enum value travels as a whole number within int64, one of the schema's
    `enumValues`, and the schema's kind says which properties are enums.
  - `@MainActor final class MirrorStore: ObservableObject` with
    `func apply(_ message: LinkMessage)`, `func object(_ key: String) -> MirrorObject?`,
    `func objects(ofClass: String) -> [MirrorObject]`, `var isSnapshotComplete: Bool`,
    `var isStale: Bool` (true from a lost link until the next snapshot completes), and
    `func write(_ key: String, property: String, value: MirrorValue) async -> PropertyWriteOutcome`.
    The store never refuses a write on its own: the wire `schema` carries only ordinal,
    name and kind, and the station refuses a write to a property it sets itself, which
    comes back as the outcome.
  - `property.write` carries a fresh nonzero uint32 `writeId`. Its outcome comes from
    the matching `property.result` entry (`property`, `accepted`, `reason`, `hasValue`,
    `value`, where `value` is what the station kept) when the agreed minor is 5 or more
    and `propertyResultVersion` is 1 or more; otherwise from the next `delta` for that
    property, as the link document describes.
  - `@MainActor final class MirrorObject: ObservableObject { let key: String; let className: String; @Published private(set) var values: [String: MirrorValue] }`
  - `@MainActor final class SettingsProxyClient: ObservableObject` with
    `func value(_ key: String) -> String?`, `func write(_ key: String, _ value: String) async -> SettingsWriteOutcome`,
    `func remove(_ key: String) async`. It writes only the keys the link document marks
    station-scoped, holds the snapshot as a local cache, and tells its own echo from
    another device's change by `origin`, which only `settings.write` and
    `settings.value` carry (the Core's own changes and every removal, this app's
    included, carry `origin` ""). A `settings.reject` puts back the value it carries; an
    operator-local key comes back with no entry.
  - `struct CommandArgument { let name: String; let value: MirrorValue }` and
    `actor CommandClient { func invoke(_ verb: String, arguments: [CommandArgument], timeout: Duration, onPhase: (@Sendable (CommandResult) -> Void)? = nil) async throws -> CommandResult }`.
    Arguments keep their order on the wire (`args` is an ordered list, and the
    conformance runner compares it in order). Ids start at 1 and run to 4294967295; in
    fixture runs they stay below the scripted ids, which start at 1000. A PureSignal
    action answers twice on one id (phase `accepted`, then `completed` or `failed`, the
    last possibly not accepted), so `invoke` resolves on the final answer and hands the
    interim one to `onPhase`.
    `CommandResult { accepted: Bool; reason: String; affectedKeys: [String]; values: [String: MirrorValue]; phase: String? }`,
    where `affectedKeys` is read from the wire key `affected`.
  - `struct StationMetrics` decoded from `station.metrics.v1`, read only when the agreed
    minor is 3 or more with telemetry version 1 or more; host fields need minor 10 and
    version 2, receivers need minor 11 and version 3. A message whose `sequence` is not
    higher than the last, or whose `sampledElapsedMs` went back, is dropped; one over
    16 KiB is refused.

**Acceptance:**
- A replayed connect sequence from the session fixtures builds the expected objects,
  classes and values, enum values as numbers; a delta changes only the named
  properties; `object.destroy` removes the object.
- A write sends `property.write` with a fresh nonzero `writeId` and resolves on the
  matching `property.result`; a refused write (a property the station sets itself, for
  example) resolves as refused with the station's reason and leaves the mirrored value as
  the station has it; below minor 5, or without `propertyResultVersion`, it resolves
  from the next `delta`.
- Settings: a write updates the cache optimistically; a `settings.reject` puts back the
  value it carries; a `settings.value` from another origin updates the cache; this
  app's own echoes and removals are recognised as the link document describes.
- `CommandClient` sends arguments in order, pairs results by command id, ignores a result
  for an unknown id, resolves a two-phase PureSignal action on its final answer
  (reporting a final answer that is not accepted), and a timeout throws without leaking
  the pending entry.
- After a lost link `isStale` is true and values stay readable; the next
  `snapshot.complete` clears it.
- A `capabilities` message after the snapshot (a changed display allowance) replaces the
  capabilities as a whole set; `capabilities` followed by `settings.snapshot` (a radio
  that arrived late) replaces the capabilities and merges the settings, never replacing
  them; the mirror clears nothing on either.
- A verb the station does not know comes back as a `command.result` that is not
  accepted, with a reason; `invoke` returns it with that reason and the session stays
  up.
- Telemetry out of sequence, or with an earlier `sampledElapsedMs`, is dropped; fields
  beyond the agreed telemetry version are not read.

**Verification:** unit and conformance. `ios/scripts/swift-test.sh --filter NereusMirrorTests`.

**Execution note (advisory):** opus. Requires Task 8.

- [ ] **Step 1:** Values, objects and the store with the replay tests.
- [ ] **Step 2:** Writes, settings, commands, telemetry and staleness.

## Task 10: WebRTC in the app

**Runs in:** the phone session, on `claude/iphone-app` (a phone task).

**Requirements:** spec §4.2 R3 (Opus audio and display endpoints over the station's
media peer), R-IOS-01 (the media plane interoperates with the station's).

**Files:**
- Create (vendored, each through `vendor-sources.sh` with a `THIRD-PARTY.md` row):
  `ios/NereusKit/Sources/CDataChannel/` (libdatachannel v0.24.5, C API `rtc/rtc.h`),
  `CJuice/` (libjuice `3c40a3545b6b1b62c7adee7f8f2bd58aa290afd6`), `CSrtp/` (libsrtp
  `24b3bf8f19b6f5ab4cd2bcceb4f4064efca86fd5`), `CUsrsctp/` (usrsctp
  `fec583d54493f879d2ae44a743423bf8a04371ab`), `CPlog/` (plog
  `94899e0b926ac1b0f4750bfbd495167b4a6ae9ef`, headers only), `CMbedTLS/` (Mbed TLS
  3.6 LTS, the newest 3.6.x release tag at the time of the task, recorded in its row).
  Archive URLs and SHA-256 values for the first five are the ones in
  `cmake/NereusRemoteMedia.cmake`.
- Create: `ios/NereusKit/Sources/NereusMedia/MediaPeer.swift`, `RtcBridge.swift`
- Create: `tests/tools/nereus_media_offerer.cpp` and its target in `tests/CMakeLists.txt`
  (a station-side helper: runs the station's `LibDataChannelMediaTransport` as the
  offering peer, exchanging signalling as JSON lines on stdin and stdout, sending
  Opus audio and NSDC frames from the conformance vectors)
- Create: `ios/scripts/interop-test.sh` (builds the helper in `build/`, then runs the
  interop tests with `NEREUS_MEDIA_OFFERER` set to its path)
- Create: `ios/NereusKit/Tests/NereusMediaTests/MediaPeerInteropTests.swift`
- Modify: `ios/NereusKit/Package.swift`, `.github/workflows/ios.yml` (an `interop` job on
  `macos-26` that builds the station helper and runs the script)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `OpusDecoder` (Task 6), `DisplayFrameDecoder` (Task 7).
- Produces:
  - `final class MediaPeer` (answering side): `init(configuration: MediaPeer.Configuration)`,
    `func setRemoteDescription(_ sdp: String) throws`, `var localDescription: AsyncStream<String>`,
    `var localCandidates: AsyncStream<(candidate: String, mid: String)>`,
    `func addRemoteCandidate(_ candidate: String, mid: String) throws`,
    `var audioPackets: AsyncStream<RtpPacket>`, `var displayDatagrams: AsyncStream<Data>`,
    `func sendAudio(_ packet: RtpPacket) throws` (used from Task 55 on),
    `var state: AsyncStream<MediaPeer.State>`, `func close()`.
  - `MediaPeer.Configuration`: `iceServers: [String]` (empty before Part E), MTU 1000,
    maximum message size 65536, host candidates only until Part E, ICE over TCP off, and
    the station's peer settings: the answer is set explicitly (`forceMediaTransport` and
    `disableAutoNegotiation` true), and the SCTP send and receive buffers (65536 and
    131072) are set once per process before the first peer.
  - Signalling within the media control document's limits: trickle candidates only (no
    candidate embedded in an SDP, which the station refuses, in the answer as in the
    offer), SDP at most 64 KiB, each candidate at most 4 KiB, a mid at most 256 bytes,
    no NUL, at most 64 remote candidates.
  - Audio arrives on the `audio` media line, send-only from the station.
    `func setExpectedAudioSsrc(_ ssrc: UInt32?)`: when set, RTP with any other SSRC is
    dropped (Task 11 sets it from each `audio-context`; the media document gives how the
    station derives it). The receive queue holds 64 packets per stream and the display
    channel keeps at most 8 messages or 256 KiB between drains, each dropping the
    oldest.
  - `struct RtpPacket { var payloadType: UInt8; var sequence: UInt16; var timestamp: UInt32; var ssrc: UInt32; var payload: Data }`.
  - libdatachannel built with Mbed TLS for DTLS (`USE_MBEDTLS=1`, `USE_NICE=0`,
    `RTC_ENABLE_MEDIA=1`, `RTC_ENABLE_WEBSOCKET=0`, `RTC_STATIC`).

**Acceptance:**
- The interop test connects `MediaPeer` to `nereus_media_offerer` on 127.0.0.1:
  DTLS completes with the Mbed TLS side answering the station's OpenSSL side; the
  display channel (label `display`, unordered, no retransmissions) delivers the
  fixture frames, which decode; Opus packets on payload type 111 arrive and decode;
  the DTLS fingerprint check is never disabled (a test with a wrong fingerprint in the
  offer fails to connect).
- The local answer carries no embedded candidates, each candidate goes out through
  `localCandidates`, and an SDP or candidate over its limit is refused before it reaches
  libdatachannel.
- With an expected SSRC set, RTP from any other SSRC is dropped; under a burst the audio
  queue and the display channel hold their bounds by dropping the oldest.
- The package builds for macOS and, from Task 51 on, for the iOS simulator and device
  SDKs; `verify-ios-provenance.py` passes with the six new rows.
- If SwiftPM cannot build usrsctp or libdatachannel for either platform with the
  defines above after following each library's own CMake option set, the task switches
  to the fallback and says so in its report: build each library with CMake for
  `CMAKE_SYSTEM_NAME=iOS` and for macOS into static libraries, combine them with
  `xcodebuild -create-xcframework` into `ios/Frameworks/NereusWebRTC.xcframework`
  (built by a committed script, never committed as a binary), and reference it from a
  `binaryTarget`. The fallback needs Xcode, which is installed (Xcode 27, 2026-09-24), so it
  runs within this task.

**Verification:** networking between two processes on loopback: integration.
`ios/scripts/interop-test.sh` passes; `ios/scripts/swift-test.sh` still passes.
